Statutory orders and notifications issued by the Ministries of the Government of India (other than 
Ministry of Defence) and by Central Authorities (other than the Administration of Union Territories) 


DELHI DEVELOPMENT AUTHORITY 

New Delhi, the 25 th September 1970 
(61 of 1957) 

Constituted under the Delhi Development 2 * 4 Act, 1957 

S.O. 1135. — In exercise of the powers conferred 
by section 57 of the Delhi Development Act, 1957 (61 
of 1957), the Delhi Development Authority with the 
previous approval of the Central Government, hereby 
makes the following regulations, namely: — 

1. Short title and application. — (i) These regulations 
may be called the Delhi Development Authority (Issue 
and Management of Bonds) Regulations, 1970. 

(ii) They shall apply to bond issued and sold by 
the Authority under sub-section (3) of Section 23 of 
the Delhi Development Act, 1957. 

2. Definitions. — In these regulations unless there la 
anything repugnant in the subject or context, 

(a) “The Act" means the Delhi Development Act, 
1957; 

(b) “The Bank” means the State Bank of India; 

(c) “Bond” means the bonds issued and sold by 
the Authority under sub-section (5) of sec- 
tion 23 of the Act; 

(d) “Authority” means the Delhi Development 
Authority constituted under the Delhi Deve- 
lopment Act, 1957 (01 of 1957), 


(0) “Defaced Bond” means a bond which has 
been made illegible and rendered undeci- 
pherable in material parts and the material 
parts of a bond are those where: — 

(i) the number, the issue to which it apper- 
tains and the face value of the bond oar 
payments of interest are recorded, or 

(ii) the endorsement or the name of the payee 
is written, or 

(iii) the renewal receipt is supplied. 

(£) “Form” means a form as set out in the sche- 
dule to these regulations. 

(g) “Lost Bond” means a bond which has 
actually been lost and shall not mean a bond 
which is in possession of some person adver- 
sely to the claimant; 

(h) “Mutilated Bond” means a bond which hasi 
been destroyed, torn or damaged in material 
parts thereof; 

( 1 ) “Office of Issue” means the Office of the State 
Bank of India, Parliament Street, New Delhi/ 
Office of the Delhi Development Authority or 
the books of which a bond is registered or 
may be registered, as the case may be; 

(j) “Prescribed Officer” means such officers of 
the Authority or the Bank as may be autho- 
rised by it for the purposes of Regulations 
9, 10, 11, 13, 14 and 15. 


( 1813 ) 



THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: MAY 20, 1972/VAISAKHA 30, 1894 


1814 


3. Form of the bond and the mode of transfer thereof 
etc, — (i) A bond may ibe issued in the form of promiss - 
ory note payable to, or to the order of, a certain person; 

(ii) A bond shall be transferable by endorsement 
and delivery like a promissory note payable to order; 

(iii) No endorsement of a bond shall bs valid unless 
made by the signature of the holder or his duly cons- 
tituted attorney or representative inscribed on the 
back of the bond itself; 

(iv) No writing on a bond is valid for the purpose 
of negotiation if such writing purports to transfer 
only part of the amount denominated by the bond; 

(v) (a) The bond shall be issued over the signature 
of the Vice-Chairman of the Authority which may be 
printed, engraved or lithographed or impressed by 
such other mechanical process as the Authority may 
direct: 

(b) A signature so printed, engraved, lithographed 
Or otherwise impressed shall be as valid as if it had 
been inscribed in the proper handwriting of the sig- 
natory himself. 

3 A. Issue of Bonds etc.— (i) The Bonds will be 
issued by the Bank. 

(Ii) A list of bonds issued by the Bank shall be 
forwarded to the office of the Authority for purposes 
of registration on Its books. 

4. Trust not recognised, — The Authority or the Bank 
shall not be bound or compelled to recognise in any 
way even when having notice thereof, any trust or 
any right in respect of a bond -other than an absolute 
right thereto in the holder. 

5. Person disqualified to be holder. — -No minor 
and no person who has been found by a competent court 
to be of unsound mind shall be entitled to be a holder. 

0, Payment of interest. — (i) Interest on a bond shall 
be paid by the office of issue or any other office of 
the Bank specified in the bond prospectus sublect to 
compliance by the holder of the bond with such for- 
malities as the office of issue may require, on presen- 
tation of the bond. 

(Ii) The interest due on the bond shall, on the 
holder complying with the formalities, be paid to him 
by issuing an Interest warrant In his favour payable at 
any of the office of the Bank specified on the Interest 
Warrant. 

7. Procedure when bond Is lost, etc. — (1) Every app- 
lication for the issue of a duplicate bond in place of 
a bond which is alleged to have been lost, stolen, des- 
troyed, mutilated or defaced, either wholly or in part 
shall be addressed to the office of issue, and shall con- 
tain the following particulars, namely: — 

(a) Particulars of the Bond according to the fol- 
lowing form: — - 
Bond for Rs 

No of the per cent Bond. . . . 


(bO Last-half-year for which interest has been 
paid; 

(c) the person to whom such interest was paid; 

(d) The person in whose name bond was issued 
(If known) : 

(e) the place of payment of interest at which the 
bond was enfaced; 

(f) The circumstances attending the loss, theft, 
destruction, mutilation or defacement; and 

(g) Whether the loss or theft was reported to the 
police 

(ii) Such applications shall be accompanied by: — 

(a), where the bond was lost In course of trans- 
mission by registered post, the Post Office 


[Part IT- 


registr at ion receipt for the letter containi--,; 
the bond; 

(b) a copy of the police report, if the loss or ihcft 
was reported to the police; 

(c) where the last payment of interest was not 
made by the office of issue, a letter signed by 
the Manager of the Bank where interest was 
last paid, certifying the last payment of inter- 
est on the bond and stating the name of the 
party to whom such payment was made; 

(d) if the applicant Is not the registered holder, 
an affidavit sworn before a magistrate testi- 
fying that the applicant was the last legal 
holder of the bond, and all documentary evi- 
dence necessary to trace back the title to 
the registered holder; and 

(e) any portion or fragments which may remain 
of the lost, stolen, destroyed, mutilated or 
defaced bond. 

(iii) A copy of the application addressed to the 
office of issue shall also lbe went to the^office of the 
Bank where interest is payable, provided that it shall 
not be necessary to send copies of enclosures accom- 
panying the application. 

8. Notification in Gazette. — The loss, theft, destruc- 
tion mutilation or defacement of a bond or portion of 
a bond shall forthwith be notified by the applicant in 
three successive issues of the Gazette of India and 
of the local office Gazette, if any, of the place where 
the loss, theft, destruction, mutilation or defacement 
occurred. Such notification shall be in the following 
form or as nearly in such form as circumstances per- 
mit:— 

“Lost”, (“stolen”, “destroyed”, “mutilated” or “de- 
faced” as the case may be). 

The Delhi Development Authortiy bond No.. , 

of the. ...... .per cent Bond for Rs ! '.origi- 
nally standing in the name of and last edor- 

sed to the proprietor, by whom, it was never 

endorsed to any other person having been lost( stolen, 
destroyed, mutilated, or defaced) notice is hereby 
given that payment of the above bond and the interest 
thereupon has been stopped at the office of issue and 
that application is about to be made or has been made 
for the issue of a duplicate in favour of the proprie- 
tor. The public are cautioned against purchasing or 
otherwise dealing with the abovementioned bond! 

Name of person notifying 
Residence. 

9. Issue of duplicate bond and taking of indemnity.— 

(1) After the publication of the last notification pres- 
cribed in regulation 8, the prescribed officer shall, if 
he Is satisfied of the loss, theft, destruction, mutilation 
or defacement of the bond and of the justice of the 
claim of the applicant, cause the particulars of the 
bond to be included in a list published under regula- 
tion 10, and shall order the office of issue: 

(a) If only a portion of the bond has been lost, 
stolen destroyed, mutilated or defaced, and if 
a portion thereof sufficient for its identifica- 
tion has been produced, to pay interest and 
to issue to the applicant, on execution of an 
indemnity bond such as is hereinafter men- 
tioned a duplicate bond in place of that of 
which a portion has been so lost, stolen destroy- 
ed, mutilated or defaced either immediately 
after the publication of the list under regu- 
lation 10 or on the expiry of such period as 
the prescribed officer may consider neces- 
sary from the date of the publication of the 
said list; 

(h) if no portion of the bond so lost, stolen des- 
troyed, mutilated or defaced, sufficient for its 
identification has been produced: 

(i) to pay to the applicant, twu years after the 
publication of the said list, and on the exe- 
cution of an indemnity bond in the manner

hereinafter prescribed, the interest in res- 
pect of the bond so lost, stolen, destroyed, 
mutilated or defaced till the expiry of the 
period of six years as next hereinafter 
provided; and 

(ii) to issue to the applicant a duplicate bond 
in place of the bond so lost, stolen, des- 
troyed, mutilated or defaced six years after 
the date of publication of the said list; pro^ 
vided that:— - 

(i) if the date on which the bond is due for 
repayment falls earlier than the date on 
which the said period of six years expires 
the prescribed officer shall, within six 
weeks of the former date, invest the prin- 
cipal amount due on the bond in the Post 
Office Savings Bank, and shall repay this 
amount, together with any interest which 
may have accrued thereon in such Bank, 
to the applicant at the time when a dup- 
licate bond would otherwise have been 
issued, and 

(ii) if at any time before the issue of the dup- 
licate bond the original bond is discovered 
or it appears to the office of issue for 
other reasons that the order should be res- 
cinded, the matter ^hall be referred to 
the prescribed officer for further consi- 
deration and in the meantime all action 
on the order shall be suspended. An 
order passed under this sub-regulation 
shall on expiry of the period of six years 
referred to therein, become final unless 
it is in the meantime rescinded or other- 
wise modified. 

(2) The prescribed officer may, at any time prior 
to the issue of a duplicate bond, if he finds sufficient 
reason, alter or cancel anv order made by him under 
this regulation and may also direct that the interval 
before the issue of a duplicate bond shall be extended 
by such period not exceeding six years as he may 
think fit. 

(3) Indemnity Bonds: — 

(i) (a) When executed under sub-regulation 
(1) (a) shall be for twice the amount of 

interest involved that is to pay, twice the 
amount of all back interest accrued due on 
the bond plus twice the amount of all interest 
to accrue due thereon during the period which 
will have to elapse before the issue of a 
duplicate bond can be made, and 

(b) in all other cases shall be for twice the face 
value of the bond plus twice the amount of 
interest calculated in accordance with clause 
(a). 

(ii) The prescribed officer may direct that such 
indemnity bond shall be executed by the ap- 
plicant alone or by the applicant and one or 
+wo sureties approved by him as he may think 
fit. to* stf; 

10. Publication of list.— (1) The list referred to in 
regulation 9 shall be published half -yearly in the 
Gazette of India in the months of January and July 
or as soon afterwards as may be convenient, 

(2) All bonds in respect of which an order has been 
passed under regulation 9 shall be included in the 
first list published next after the passing of such order 
and thereafter such bonds shall continue to bp includ- 
ed in every succeeding list until tne expiration of 
six years from the date of first publication. 

(31 The list shall contain the following particulars 
regarding each bond included therein, namely, the 
name of the issue, the number of the bond, its value, 
t,hp name of the person to whom it was issued, the 
date from which it bears interest, 1 ue name of the 
applicant for a duplicate the number and date of the 


order passed by the prescribed officer for payment 
of interest or issue of a duplicate and the date of 
publication of the list in which the bond was first 
included, 

11. Determination of a mutilated bond is a bond 
requiring renewal. — It shall be at the option of the 
prescribed officer to treat a bond which has been muti- 
lated or defaced as a bond requiring issue of a dup- 
licate under regulation 9 or mere renewal under re- 
gulation 14, 

12. When a bond required to be renewed. — (1) A 

holder of a bond may be required by the office of 
issue to receipt the same for renewal in any of the 
following cases, namely: 

(a) if only sufficient room remains on the back 
of the bond for one further endorsement or 
if any word is written upon the bond across 
the existing endorsement or endorsements; 

(b) if the bond is torn or any way damaged or 
crowded with writing or unfit, in the opinion 
of the office of issue’ 

(c) if any endorsement is not clear and distinct or 
does not indicate the payee or payees, as the 
case may be, by name or is made otherwise 
than in one of the endorsement cages on the 
back of the bond; 

(d) If the Interest on the bond has remained 
undrawn for ten years or more; 

(e) if the interest cages on the reverse of the 
bond have been completely filled or if the 
vacant prescribed cages on the reverse of the 
bond do not correspond with the half years 
for which interest has become due on the date 
when the bond is presented for drawal of 
interest; 

(f) if the bond having been enfaced three times 
for payment of interest is presented for rc- 
enfpeement; and 

(g) if in the opinion of the office of issue, the 
title of the person presenting the bond for 
payment of Interest is irregular or not fully 
proved 

(2) When requisition for renewal of a bond has 
been made under sub-regulation (1) payment of any 
further interest thereon shall be refused until it is re- 
ceipted for renewal and actually renewed. 

13. Person whose title to a bond of a deceased sole 
holder may he recognised. — (1) The executors or ad- 
ministrators of a deceased sole holder of a bond (whe- 
ther a Hindu, Mohammedan, Parsi or otherwise) and 
the holder of a succession cert it jot,-: issued under 
Part X of the Indian Succession Act, 1925 (XXXIX of 
1925) in respect of the bond shall be the only persons 
who may be recognised by the office of issue (subject 
to any general or special instructions of the pres- 
cribed officer) as having any title to the bond. 

(2) Notwithstanding anything contained in Section 
45 of the Indian Contract Act, 1872 (IX of 1872), in 
the case of a bond issued, sold or held payable to 
two or more holders, tfie survivors or survivor and 
on the death of th« last survivor, his executors, admi- 
nistrators, or any person who Is the holder of a succes- 
sion certificate in respect of such bond shall be the 
only person who may be recognised by the office of 
issue (subject to any general or special instructions 
of the prescribed officer) as having any title to the 
bond. 

(3) The office of issue shall not be bound to re- 
cognise such executors or administrators unless they 
shall have obtained probate or letters of administra- 
tion or other legal representation as the case may be 
from a competent court or office in India, having 
effect at the place of sil nation of the office of issue 
Provided nevertheless thar in any case where the 
prescribed officer shall in his absolute discretion

think fit, it shall be lawful for him to dispense with

or other legal representation upon such terms as to

14- Receipt for renewal etc. — (1) Subject to any 
general or special instructions of the prescribed 
officer, the office of issue, by its order, on the appli- 
cation of the holder, on his delivering the bond or 
bonds and on his satisfying the office of issue regard- 
ing the justice of his claim, renew, sub-divide or con- 
solidate a bond or bonds provided the bond or bonds 
has or have been receipted in Form I, II, or III as 
the case may be. 

(2) The office of issue may, under the orders of 
prescribed officer, require the applicant for renewal, 
sub-division or consolidation of a bond under the sub- 
regulation (1) to execute a bond in Form IV with one 
or mote sureties approved by him. 

15. Renewal of bond In case of dispute as to title. — ■ 
Where there is a dispute as to the title to a bond in 
respect of which an application for renewal has been 
made, the prescribed officer may: — 

(a) where any party to the dispute has obtained 
a final decision from a court of competent 
jurisdiction declaring him to be entitled to 
such bond, issue a renewed bond in favour 
of such party; or 

(b) refusn to renew the bond until such a deci- 
sion has be$n obtained. 

Explanation. — For the purposes of this sub -regulation, 
the expression 'final decision’ means a decision which 
is not appealable or a decision which is appealable but 
against which no appeal has been filed within the 
period of limitation allowed by law. 

16. Liability in respect of bond renewed etc. — When 
a duplicate bond has been issued under I'Ogulatirm 9 
or a renewed bond has been issued or a new bond 
has been issued upon sub-division or consolidation 
under regulation 14, in favour of a person, the bond 
so issued shall be deemed to constitute a new coti tract 
between the Authority and such person and all per- 
sons deriving title thereafter through him. 

17. Discharge. — The Authority shall be discharged 
from all liability in respect of the bond or bands paid 
on maturitv or in place of which a duplicate, renewed, 
sub-divided or consolidated bond or bonds has or have 
been issued: — 

(aj in the case of pavment. after the lapse of six 
years from the date on which payment was 
due; 

(b) in the case of a duplicate bond after tbe lapse 
of six years from the date of the publication 
under regulation 10 of the list in which the 
bond is first mentioned, or from the date of 
the payment of interest on the original bond, 
whichever date is later; 

(c) in the case of a renewed bond or of r new 
bond issued upon-sub-division nr consolida- 
tion after thp lapse of six years from the date 
of issue thereof. 

18. THychura-e in rewpeot of Interest, — Sav e eg other- 
wise expre^shr provided m th^ terms of the bond, no 
person shall be entitled to claim interest on anv such 
bond in respect of anv Period which has elapsed after 
the earliest date on which demand could have been 
made fqr the payment of the amount due on such 
bond. 

19. Discharge of a bond. — When a bond becomes due 
for oaympiit of orinHnal, the hood shall be presented 
at the office of the Ra^k at which the interest thereon 
is oayahlp or at the office of issue duly signed by the 
holder on its reverse. 

20. Acceptable as Security — The bonds shall he nc- 

rpotable to the Authority r<? security otherwise re- 
ouirod to he deposited in cash hy its employees, con- 
tractor* or suppliers and other persons who might 
transact with the Authority.

JUDGMENT: (Oral), 

By my order dated November 15, 1971, I had found 
that the selected ballot papers do not contain any of 
the characteristics which were alleged by the petitioner 
and the inspection of the ballot papers must rest there. 
The request of the petitioner for further chemical exa- 
mination was therefore, rejected and it was directed 
that the petitioner will not be entitled to lead any 
evidence on Issues Nos. 8 to 14. 

This petition has been listed for today for recording 
evidence on behalf of the petitioner oh Issues Nos. 1 
to 7 which related to other corrupt practices. The 
petitioner has made a statement in Court today stating 
that all that he is interested in showing Is that a fraud 



i8i8 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: MAY 20, 1P72/VAI SAKHA 30, 1G94 


[Paht li- 


as alleged by him had been perpetrated and he does 
not want to give any evidence with regard to Issues 
Nos. 1 to 7. On a question being put by me, he has 
cateKorlcallv stated that he has not entered into any 
bargain with and no consideration has passed between 
him and respondent No. 1 leading to his statement that 
he does not want to lead any evidence in support of 
Issues Nos. 1 to 7. I am satisfied from the statement 
of the petitioner that there has been no bargain with 
and consideration passed between him and respondent 
No, I. 

In view of section, 110 of the Representation to the 
People Act, 1951, I had asked the learned counsel for 
both sides to show me cases to the effect that the pro- 
visions of the Code of Civil Procedure wj.ll apply to 
the trial of an election petition Including the provisions 
of dismissal of an election petition for default unless 
there Is a nrovlsion contrary thereto iff the said Act. I 
had asked them to do so becaus^ in the event on elec- 
tion petitioner applies for withdrawal, an elaborate 
procedure r* flailed in section 110 of the said Act has 
to be gone through. 

Counsel on both sides have cited to me the cases re- 
ported in A.I.R. 1953 Madhya Pradesh 260 in re: Sunder- 
Jal Mannalal v. Nandramdas Dwarkadas and others; 
AJJi. 1965 Patna 378 in re: Sawalia Behari L all Verma 
v.Tr/bikram Deo Naraln Smgh and others and A.I.R. 
1968 Punjab and Haryana 152 in re: Jugal Kishore v. 
Doctor Balder Prakash in which It has been held that 
an election petition can be dismissed for default of 
appearance. The situation that has arised before me 
is not that the petitioner has defaulted jn appearance 
but that having appeared, he has stated that he Is not 
interested in leading evidence on Issues Nos. 1 to 7 
because he would like to agitate* the question relating 
to Issues Nos. 8 to 14 in the Supreme court where he 
had filer.] a petition for special leave to appeal against 
my order dated November 15, 1971 which had been 
with drawn in the circumstances stated by the petitioner 
in hi statement today, 

Fqt' In cfc of evidence on Issues Nos, 1 to 7, the onus 
of which was upon the petitioner it has to be held that 
the petitioner has failed to substantiate any of thcic 
Issues which must be decided against him for this very 
reason. It is not necessary to call upon respondent No. 
1 to furnish any evidence on these Issues. 

The result, therefore, is that Issues Nos. [ t 0 7 are 
decided against the petitioner. 

As to Issue No. 15, is Its consideration ls-rendered un- 
necessary because paragraph 20 of the petition which 


gave rise to this Issue was connected with the lasuos 
relating to fraudulent ballot papers. Therefore, no 
order is' necessary on this Issue. 

Issue No. 16 also depends on whether or not a lac 
or more fraudulent votes were counted for respondent 
No. 1 in the election and this Issue is ajso, therefore, 
connected with the Issues relating to fraudulent ballot 
papers and falls with Issues Nos. 8 to 14. 

The corrupt practice which were the subject, matter 
of the Issues framed by me were as follows: — 

(1) use and counting of chemically trated ballot 
paper? numbering about a lac or more in 
favour of respondent No. 1; 

(2) bribery and payment of cash money for secur- 
ing votes (Issue No. 1); 

(3) appeal to voters on the ground of religion; 
caste; community and language (Issue No. 2); 

(4) Appeal to voters in the name of cow and calf 
as a religious symbol (Issue No. &);- 

(5) obtaining or procuring assistance of gazetted 
officers, piembers“of the Armed forces of the 
Union; members of the Police Force and Excise 
Officers (Issue No. 4); 

(6) incurring or authorising expenditure in excess 
of section 77 (Issues Nos. 5 and 0) and 

(7) attacking the personal character or conduct of 
the petitioner (Issue No. 7). 

None of these Issues has been proved. 

The result is that the election petition is dismissed 
In view of the fact that the petition has not gone into 
full trial, and no evidence has been recorded, the order 
as to costs will bp that the petitioner, will pay Rs. 500/- 
ns costs to respondent No. 1. 

Intimation of this decision t obe sent by the office 
as required under section 103 of the Act, 

March 13, 1972. 

(Sd.) S. N. Andley, 
JUDGE.

ORDER 

Nflir Delhi, the 17th February 1971 

S.O. 11 39. — In exercise of the powers conferred bv 
sect on 3 of the Essential Commodities Act. 1955 (10 
of 1965), the Central Government hereby make* the 
following Order further to amend the Toxti.es (Pro- 
duction by Power-loom) Control Order, 1956, name- 
ly:- 

1. (1) This Order may be called the Textile* (Pro- 
duction by Power-loom) Control (1st Amendment) 
Order, 1972. 

(2) It shall come into force atonce. 

f 

X Jn the textiles (Produ^ion by Power-loom) Con- 
trol Order. 1956 (hereinafter referred to as the said 
Ord°r). clause 6 shall be renumbered as sub-clause (1) 
of that clause, and after sub-clause (1) as so renum- 
bered. the following sub-clause shall be inserted, 
namely: — 

"(2) While prantmg the permission under sub- 
clause (1), the Textile Commissioner shall 
specify — 

(a) the type of yam to be used on tuch 
power-loom, and 

(b) the Reed Soac^ cf the loom to be acquir- 
ed or installed," 


I® 27 


3. In clause 6A of the said Order — 

(i) for the opening paragraph, the following para- 
graph shall be substituted, namely: — 

“No person, whether by himself or jointly with 
others, except with the previous permis- 
sion of the Textile Commissioner, shall 
weave cloth out of any yam — 

(a) in the case of a power-loom existing 
at the commencement of the Textiles 
(Production by Power-loom) Control 

( Amendment) Order, 1971, 

other than the yam on which the 
power-loom was working immediate- 
ly before such commencement; and 

(b) in any other case, other than the 
yam specified under sub-clause (2) of 
clause 6 by the Textile Commissioner." 

(ii) for Explanation II. the following Explanation 
shall be substituted, namely: — 

“£xplannt : on n . — A certificate issued by the 
Textile Commissioner or the State Gov- 
ernment or pnv officer authrised in writ- 
ing by the Textile Commissioner or the 
State Government in For™ C as regards 
the tyDe of yarn used on the power-looms 
shall be considered sufficient proof for the 
purposes of this clause", 

4, Clause 6B of the said Order shall be omitted. 

6, In clause 7 of the said order, for the word and 
figure “clause 6", the word, figures and letter "clauses 
6, 0A" shall be substituted. 

0. In Form C appended to the said Order — 

(1) for the words, figures and letters “during the 
period of one year ending on the 31st July, 
1956", the following shall be substituted, 
namely: — 

"durine the period of one year ending on the 
31st Julv, 1956 the per'od immediately 
before the commencement of the Textiles 
(Production by Power-loom) Control 

( Amendment) Order, 1971".

thp StE»t,e Governm'n + ", the words “Signature 
of the Officer*' shall be substituted,

their favour should not be cancelled on the ground 
that they had no facilities for service and mainte-

not to <erve the purpose for which it was issued.

service and maintenance facilities in their factory,

and has come to the conclusion that the licence in 
question will not serve the purpose for which it was 
issued.

licence In question should be cancelled or otherwise 
rendered ineffective. Therefore, the undersigned, in 
exercise of powers vested in him under clause 9 sub-

S.O, 1153.— M/s, New India Dye Chem Industries, 
14/4 Mathura Road, Near Village-Mevla, Maharaipur, 
Farldahad were granted import licence No. P/S/ 
1693070/ C/XX/3S/D/31-32 dated 20th March, 1971, 
for Rs> 26,500 for Import of Crude Drugs for A.M. 71 
licensing period, They have applied for issue of 
duplicate copy of Exchange Control Purposes Copy 
thereof on the ground that original copy has been lost/ 
misplaced having been utilised partly/having been 
registered with customs authority. 

2. The applicant have filed an affidavit in support of 
their contention as required under para 312(2) of 

I.T.C. Hand Book of Rules and Procedure, 1971. I 
am satisfied that the original exchange control pur- 
poses copy of the licence in question has been lost./ 
misplaced. 

3. In exercise of the powers conferred on me under 
Section 9(cc) Import (Control) Order, 1055 dated 7th 
December, 1955, I order the cancellation of exchange 
control copy of licence No. P/S/ 1693070 dated 20th 
March. 1971. 

4. The applicant is now being issued duplicate copy 
of Exchange Control Purposes copy of the licence in 
accordance with the provision of para 312(3) of I.T.C. 
Hand Book of Rules & Procedure, 1971.

(Office of the Chief Controller of 
Imports and Exports) 

ORDER 

New Delhi , the 14 th December 1971 

S.O. 1156. — The Oil and Natural Gas Commission, 
Tel Bhavan, Dehradun were granted licence No, G/A/ 
1037490/R/ AX/ 3G/H/27-28, dated 3rd March, I960, 
against V Exim Credit for import of Hydro Carbon 
Mixtures etc. valued at Rs. 3,164. They have now 
requested for the issue of duplicate copies of the licence 
on the ground that the original licence in duplicate has 
been lost. It has been further reported by the licensee 
that the licence was lost after the utilisation of Rs. Nil 
and that without having been registered with any Cus- 
tom Authority. 

I support of their contention the applicant has 
filed an affidavit. The undersigned is satisfied that 
the original licence No. G/A/ 1037490 dated 3rd March, 
1069, in duplicate has been lost and directs that a dupli- 
cate (both copies) of the licence should be issued to 
them. The original licence is cancelled, A duplicate 
licence is being issued, separately. 

TNo. Cent./474/68.69/PLS/B.] 
SARDUL SINGH, 

Dy. Chief Controller of Imports & Exports.

(Office of tfie Chief Controller of

ORDER 

Neio Delhi J the 2nd February 1972 

S.O, 1157. — M/s, Auto Rental Service, New Delhi-1 
were granted an import licence No. P/A/1320693/C/ 
XX/35/11/30-31/ILS, dated 23rd May, 1970 for Rs, 3,000 
(Rs. three thousand only). They have applied for 
the issue of a duplicate copy of the said licence on the 
ground that the original licence has been misplaced. 
It is further stated that the licence was registered with 
the foreign post office. New Delhi and utilised for R#. 
1500 (Rs. one thousand and five hundred only), 

2, In support of this contention, the applicant has 
filed an affidavit. I am accordingly satisfied that the 
licence has been lost. Therefore, in exercise of th© 
Powers conferred under sub-clause 9(cc) of the Imports 
(Control) Order 1955 dated 7th December, 1955 ai 
amended, the said original licence No. P/A/1326003/C/ 
XX/35/H/30-31/ILS dated 23rd May. 1970 issued to 
M/s. Auto Rental Service, New Delhi is hereby cancel- 
led. 

3. A duplicate copy of the said licenc© is being issued 
separately to the licensee.

(Office of th„ Chief Controller of Imports & Exports) 
ORDER 

New Delliij the 19 th February 1972 

S.CL 1158— The Chief Electrical Engineer, Assam 
State Electricity Board, shillong were granted an im- 
port licence No. G/HP/2075590/R/IB/25/C/H/25/CU- 
II dated 18th August, 1087 fc^ Ra. 35,390/- (Rupees 
thirty five thousand three hundred and ninety only.) 
They have applied for the issue of a duplicate Customs 
Purposes copy of the said licence on the ground that 
the original Customs Purposes copy has been lost/ 
misplaced. It is further stated that the original Cus- 
toms Purposes Copy was registered with Bombay Cus- 
toms and utilised partly. It was utilised lor Rs 32,695 
and the balance available on it was Rs. 2,695 only. 

2. In support of this contention, the applicant has 
tiled an affidavit. I am accordingly satisfied that the 
original Customs Purposes Copy of thu said licence has 
been lost. Therefore, in exercise of the powers con- 
ferred under Sub-clause 9(CC) of the Imports 

(Control) Order 1955 dated 7th December, 195? as 
amended, the said original Customs Purposes Copy 
of the licence Nc. G/HP/2O7550O/R/IR/25/C/H/25/ 
CG-II dated 18th August, 1907 issued to The Chief 
Electrical Engineer, Assam State Electricity Board, 
Shillong is hereby cancelled. 

3, A duplicate Customs Purposes Copy of the said 
licence is being issued separately to the licensee. 

[No. CG-II/HEP/A-H/07-68-] 

I. A, RASHID, 

Dy. Chief Controller of Imports and Exports.

MINISTRY OF LABOUR AND REHABILITATION 
(Department of Labour and Employment) 

New Delhi , the 24th February 1972 

v S.O. 1159. — Whereas by the notification of the Gov- 
ernment of India in the Ministry of Labour and Reha- 
bilitation (Department of Labour & Employment) 
Ho, S.O. 3379, dated the 30th August, 1971, the Central 
' tovernment had declared the iron ore mining industry 
to be a public utility service for the purpose of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), for a 
period of six months from the 4th September, 1971; 

And whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that public interest requires the extension of the said 
period by a further period of six months; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by the proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause (n) of sec- 
tion 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), 
the Central Government hereby declares the said indus- 
try to be a public utility service for the purposes of 
the said Act for a further period of six months from 
the 4th March, 1972. 

[No. F. S. 11025/5/72-LR.L]

ORDERS 

New Delhi , the 17 th January 1972 

S.O. 1160. — Whereas an industrial dispute exists 
between the employers in relation to the Central 
^ank of India, New Delhi and its workmen repre- 
sented by the Central Bank Employees Union, Delhi; 

And, whereas the said employers and workmGtt 
have by a written agreement in pursuance of the 
provisions of sub-section (1) of section 10 A of the 
Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), agreed to 
refer the said dispute to arbitration by person 
specified therein, and a copy of the said arbitration 
agreement has been forwarded to the Central Gov- 
ernment; 

Now, therefore, in pursuance of the provisions of 
sub-section (3) of section 10A of the said Act, the 
Central Government hereby publishes the said arbi* 
tration agreement which was received by it on the 
6th January. 1972. 

Agreement 

(Under Section 10A of the Industrial Disputes Act, 
1947) 

Between: 

Names of Parties: 

Representing employers — Shri M. L. Bhalla, 
Chief Agent, Central Bank of India, Parlia- 
ment Street, New Delhi. gli 

Representing the workman . — Shri Tara Chand 
Gupta, General Secretary, Central Bank 


Employees Union, Central Bank Building, 
Chandni Chowk, Delhi-6. 

It is hereby agreed between the parties to refer 
the following dispute to the arbitration of Shri V. F. 
Gupta, Regional Labour Commissioner (Central) 
Shram Shakti Bhawan, Raft Marg, New Delhi. 

(i) Specific matters in disputes . — “Whether on 
the basis of the duties performed by Shri 
*M. P. Jain, Assistant Head Cashier, at IBC 
Cash Counter of Chandhi Chowk, Branch, he 
is entitled to be designated as -a Head Cashier, 
and, if so, to which category of Head 
Cashiers he should be classified." 

(ii) Details of the parties to the dispute includ- 

ing the name and address of establishment 
or undertaking Involved .— Management of 

Central Bank of India, Parliament Street, 
New Delhi and Central Bank Employees 
Union. 

(iii) Total number of workmen employed in the 
undertaking , — 240. 

(iv) Name of the union f if any representing the 
workman in question.— Central Bank Em- 
ployees Union. 

(v) Estimated number of workmen affected or 
likely to be affected by the dispute .— 1 (one). 

We further agree that the majority decisions of the 
arbitrators shall be binding on us. 

The arbitrator shall make his award within a 
period of 2 months or within such further time as 
is extended by mutual agreement between us in 
writing. In case the award is not made within the 
period aforementioned, the reference to arbitration 
shall stand automatically cancelled and we shall be 
free to negotiate for fresh arbitration. 

Witnesses: 

1 . Sd/- Uligible. Representing 

2. Sd/- Illigible. Employer. 

Sd/- M. L. Bhalla. 

Representing workman 

Sd/* Tara Chand Gupta.

New Delhi , the 22nd January 1972 

S.O, 1161 . — Whereas the Central Government is of 
opinion that an industrial dispute exists between the 
employers in relation to the State Bank of Patiala 
and their workmen in respect of the matter specified 
in the Schedule hereto annexed; 

And, whereas the Central Government considers it 
desirable to refer the said dispute for adjudication; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers con- 
ferred by section 7A, and clause (d) of sub-section 
(11 of section 10 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 
(14 of 1947), the Central Government hereby cons- 
titutes an Industrial Tribunal of which Shri P. P. R. 
Sawhney shall be the Presiding Officer, with head- 
quarters at Chandigarh and refers the said dispute 
for adjudication to the said Tribunal. 

Schedule 

Whether the action of the management of State 
Bank of Patiala in terminating the services of Shri 
Mohinder Singh, Armed Guard at their Doraha 
Branch with effect from the 8th May, 1968 is justified? 
If not, to what relief is he entitled?

New Delhi , the 25th February 1972 

S.O. 1189. — Whereas Messrs India Reinsurance Cor- 
poration Limited, Industrial Assurance Building, 
Churchgate, Bombay_20 (hereinafter referred to as the 
said establishment) has applied for exemption undei 
clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 ot the Em- 
ployees’ Provident Funds and Faanily Pension Fund 
Act, 1952 fl 9 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Govern- 
ment, the ru.es of the provident fund of the said estab- 
lishment with respect to the rates of contribution are 
not less favourable to the employees therein than those 
specified in section 6 of the said Act. and the employees 
are also in enjoyment of other provident fund benefits 
which on the whole are not less favourable to the em- 
ployees than the benefits provided under the said Act 
or under the Employees’ Provident Funds Scheme, 1952 
(hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in relation 
to tho employees in any other establishment of a 
similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the 
said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government 
hereby exempts the said establishment from the opera- 
tion of all the provisions of the said Scheme and in 
pursuance of sub-section (3) of the said section 17, the 
Central Government hereby directs that,— 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establish- 
ment shall pay within fifteen days of the close 
of the month to the Employees’ Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the fate of 0.09 
per cent (zero point zero nine per cent) of the 
pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retain- 
ing allowance, if any, and cash value of food 
concession admissible thereon) for the time 
being payable to the employees of the said 
cstabliTOment who would have become mem- 
bers under the said Scheme but for this 
exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident 
fund contributions in accordance with the 
directions issued by the Central Government 
from time to time. 

The Schedule 

1. The employer shall submit such returns to the 

Regional Provident Fund Commissioner as the 
Central Government may from time to time 
prescribe. 

2. The employer shall furnish to each 1 employee an 

Annual Statement of Account or Pass Book. 



Sec. 3(H)] 


THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: MAY 20, 1972/VAISAKHA 30, 1304 


1851 


3. All expanses involved in the administration of 

the Fund including the maintenance of 
accounts, submission of accounts and returns, 
transfer of accumulations, payment of ins- 
pection charges etc. shall be borne by the 
empkyer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board 

of the establishment a copy of the rules of the 
Fund as approved by the appropriate Govern- 
ment and, as and when amended alongwilh a 
translation of the salient, points thereof in the 
language of the majority of th? employees. 

f). Where an employee who is already members of 
the Employees’ ‘Provident Fund (Statutory 
Fund) or the Provident Fund of another ex- 
empted establishment is employed in his 
establishment* the employer shall immediately 
enrol him as a member of the Fund" of the 
establishment, and accept the past accumula- 
tions in respect of such employee and credit 
to^ his account. 

The employers shall enhance the rate o’f provident 
fund contribution appropriately if the rate of 
provident fund contributions for the class of 
establishments in which his establishment 
falls is enhanced under the Employees’ Provi- 
dent Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 
so that the benefits under the provident fund 
scheme of the establishment, shall hot become 
less favourable than the benefit provided under 
the Employees’ Provident Funds and Family 
Pension Fund Art, 1952. 

7, The establishment shall submit an audited 
balance sheet of its provident fund" every year 
to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner 
within 3 months of the close of the year, 

3, No amendment of the rules of the provident fund 
shall be made without the previous approval 
of the Regional Provident Fund Comrrtftsioner, 
■where any amendment Is likely to affect 
adversely the interests of the employee, ibe 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner shall, 
before giving his approval, give a reasonable 
opportunity to the employees to explain their 
point of view*

inafter referred to as the said establishment) hus 
applied for exemption under clause (a) of sub-section 
(1) of section 17 of the Employees’ Provident Funds 
and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Govern- 
ment the rules of the provident fund of the said 
establishment with respect to the rates of contribution 
are not less favourable to the employees therein than 
those specified in section 6 of the said Act, and the 
employees arc also in enjoyment of other provident 
fund benefits which on the whole are not less favour- 
able to the employees than the benefits provided under 
the said Act or under the Employees* Provident Funds 
Scheme, 1952 (hereinafter referred to as the said 
Scheme) in relation to the employees in any other 
establishment of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the 
said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government 
hereby exempts the said establishment from the opera- 
tion of all the provisions of the said Scheme and in 
pursuance of sub-section (3) of the said section 17, the 
Central Government hereby directs that, — 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establish- 
ment shall pay within fifteen days of the 
close of the month to the Employees* Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0.09 
per cent (zero point zero nine per cent) of the 
pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retaining 
allowance, if any, and cash value of fond 
concession admissible thereon) for the time 
being payable to the employees of the paid 
establishment who would have become mem- 
bers under the said Scheme but for this 
exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident 
fund contribution^ in acordance with the dlrei> 
tlons issued by the Central Government from 
time to time. 

The Schedule 

1, The employer shall submit such returns to the 
Regional ^rovident Fund Commissioner as 


the Central Government may, from time to 
time, prescribe, 

2, The employer shall furnish to each employee an 

Annual Statement of Account or Pass Book. 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of 

the Fund including the maintenance of 
accounts, submission of accounts and returns, 
transfer of accumulations, payment of inspec- 
tion charges, etc, shall be borne by the em- 
ployer. 

4, The employer shall display on the Notice Board 

of the establishment a copy of the rules of 
the Fund as approved by the appropriate 
Government and, as and when attended, 
alongwith a translaion of the salient points 
thereof in the language of the majority of the 
employees. 

5. Where an employee who is already member of 

the Employees' Provident Fund (Statutory 
Fund) or the Provident Fund of another 
exempted establishment Is employed In bis 
establishment, the employer shall immediately 
enrol him as a member of the Fund of the 
establishment, and accept the past accumula- 
tions In respect of such employee and credit to 
his account. 

6 .The employer shall enhance the rate of provi- 
dent fund contribution appropriately if the 
rate of provident fund contributions for the 
class of establishments in which his establish- 
ment falls is enhanced under the Employees’ 
Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 
1952 so that the benefits under the provident 
fund scheme of the establishment shall not 
become less favourable than the benefit pro- 
vided under the Employees’ Provident Funds 
and Family Pension Fund Act, 3952. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audit 

balance sheet of its provident fund every year 
to the Regional Provident Fund Commissioner 
within 3 months of the close of the year. 

8 . No amendment of the Rules of the Provident 

f”nd shall he made without the previous 
approval of the Central provident Fund Com- 
mission er, Where any amendment is Jlkelv 
to affect adversely the Interests of the em- 
ployees, the Central Provident Fund Commis- 
sioner shall before giving his approval, give a 
reasonable opportunity to the employees to 
explain their point of view.

S.O. 1191. — Whereas exemption was granted to 
Messrs Bengal Enamel Works Limited, Fait a, District 
24-Fa*rganas, West Bengal (hcreinatfer called the said 
Establishment) under Clause (a) of sub-section (1) of 
Sestion 17 of the IlmployeeR , Provident Funds and 
Family Pension Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952), by the 
notification of the Government of India in the late 
Ministry of Labour and Employment, number 3*0. 
3774, dated the 7th December, 1902, subject to the 
conditions specified in tha said notification; 

And whereas the said Establishment have com- 
mitted the following defaults, namely: — 

(i) failure to submit returns In time; 

(ii) delay in payment of inspection charges; 

(iii) failure to transfer provident fund money to 
the Board of trustees. 

And whereas the said Establishment by reason of 
the commission of the aforesaid defaults have failed 
to comply with the conditions subject to which the 
exemption was granted to the Establishment; 

And whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
that the failure to comply with the conditions has. con- 
tinued without any sufficient cause inspite of reason- 
able opportunity having being given to the said Estab- 
lishment to comply with those conditions; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
bv sub-section (4) of section 17 of the said Act, the 
Central Government hereby cancels, with immediate 
effect, the exemption granted to the said Establishment.

S.O. 1193, — ^Whereas Messrs. Devkaran Nanjee Insu- 
rance Company Limited, Devkaran Building Harninun 
Circle, Bombay (hereinafter referred to as the said 
establishment) has applied for exemption under 
clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the 
Employees* Provident Fund3 and Family 1 Pension Fund 
Act, 1952 (19 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Govern- 
ment, the rules of the provident fund of the said establi- 
shment with respect to the rates of contribution are 
not less favourable to the employees therein than those 
specified in section 6 of the said Act, and the employees 
are also in enjoyment of other provident fund benefits 
which on the whole are not less favourable to the 
employees than the benefits provident under the said 
Act or under the Employees’ Provident Funds Scheme, 
1952 (hereinafter referred to as the sadd Scheme) in 
relation to the employees in any other establishment 
of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 of the 
said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government 
hereby exempts the said establishment from the opera- 
tion of all the provisions of the said Scheme and in 
pursuance of sub-section (3) of the said section 17, 
the Central Government hereby directs that, — 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establish- 
ment shall pay within fifteen days of the close 
of the month to the Employees’ Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0,09 
per cent (zero point zero nine per cent) of the 
pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retain- 
ing allowance, if any, and cash value of 
food Concession admissible thereon) for the 
time being payable to the employees of Ihe 
said establishment who would have become 
members under the said Scheme but for this 
exemption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident 
fund contributions in accordance with the 
directions Issued by the Central Government 
from time to time. 

The Schedule 

1- The employer shall submit such returns to the 
Regional Provident Fund Commissioner as the Central 
Government may, from time to time, prescribe, 

2* The employer shall furnish to each employee an 
Annual Statement of Account or Pass Book, 


3- All expenses involved in the administration of the 
Fund including lh© maintenance of accounts, submis- 
sion of accounts and returns, transfer of accumulations, 
payment of inspection charges etc, shall be borne by 
the employer. 

4. The Employer shall display on the Notice Board 
of the establishment a copy of the Buies of the Fund 
as approved by the appropriate Government and, as 
and when amended alongwith a translation of the 
salient points thereof in the language of the majority 
of the employees, 

5. Where an employee who is already member of the 
Employees' Provident Fund (Satutory Fund) of the 
Provident Fund of another exempted establishment is 
employed in his establishment, the employer shall im- 
mediately enrol him as a member of the Fund of the 
establishment, and accept the past accumulation^ in 
respect of such employee and credit to his account. 

6. The employer shall enhance the rate of provident 
fund contributions appropriately if the rate of provi- 
dent Fund Contributions for the class of establishments 
in which his establishment falls is enhanced under the 
Employees’ Provident Funds t*nd Family Pension Fund 
Act, 1952 so that the benefits under the provident 
fund Scheme of the establishment shall not become 
less favourable than the benefit provided under the 
Employ eos 1 Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund 
Act, 1952. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited balance 
sheet of Its provident fund every year to the Regional 
Provident Fund Commissioner within 3 months of the 
close of the year. 

8. No amendment of the Rules of the provident fund 
shall be ma*de without the previous approval of the 
Central Provident Fund Commissioner. Where any 
amendment is likely to effect adversely the interests of 
the employees, the Central Provident Fund Commis- 
sioner shall, before giving his approval, give a reason- 
able opportunity to the employees to explain their noiet 
of view.

SO. 1194. — whereas Messrs Hindustan Antibio- 
tics Limited, Pimpiri, Poona- 13 (hereinafter referred 
to as the said establishment) has applied for exemp- 
tion under clause (a) of sub-section (1) of section 17 
of the Employees 7 Provident Funds and Family Pen- 
sion Fund Act, 1952 (19 of 1952); 

And whereas in the opinion of the Central Govern- 
ment, the rules of the provident fund of the said estab- 
lishment with respect to the rate of contribution are 
not less favourable to the employees therein than those 
specified in section 6 of the said Act, and the employees 
are also in enjoyment of other provident fund benefits 
which on the whole are not less favourable to the 
employees than the benefits provided under the said 
Act or under the Employees' Provident Funds Scheme, 
1952 (hereinafter referred to as the said Scheme) in 
relation to the employees in any other establishment 
of a similar character; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by clause (a) of sub -section (1) of section 17 of the 
said Act, and subject to the conditions specified in the 
Schedule annexed hereto, the Central Government here- 
by exempts the said establishment from the operation 
oi all the provisions of the said Scheme and in pur- 
suance of sub-section (3) of the said scetion 17, the 
Central Government hereby directs that, — 

(a) the employer in relation to the said establish- 
ment shall pay within fifteen days of the close 
of the month to the Employees 7 Provident 
Fund, inspection charges at the rate of 0.09 
per cent (zero point zero nine per cent) of the 
pay (basic wages, dearness allowance, retain- 
ing allowance, if any, and cash value of food 
concession admissible thereon) for the time 
being payable to the employees of the said 
establishment who would have become mem- 
bers under the said Scheme but for this ex- 
emption; 

(b) the said employer shall invest the provident 
fund contributions in accordance with the 
directions issued by the Central Government 
from lime to time. 

The Schedule 

1. The employer shall submit such returns to the 

Regional Provident Fund Commissioner as the 
Central Government may, from time to time, 
prescribe. 

2. The employer shall furnish to each employee 

an Annual Statement of Account or Pass Book, 

3. All expenses involved in the administration of 

the Fund including the maintenance of ac- 
counts, submission of accounts and returns, 
transfer of accumulations, payment of inspec- 
tion charges etc., shall be borne by the em- 
ployer. 

4. The employer shall display on the Notice Board 

of the establishment a copy of the Rules of 
the Fund as approved by the appropriate Gov- 
ernment and, as and when amended, along- 
with a translation of the salient points thereof 
in the language of the majority of the cm- 
polyces. 

5. Where an employee, who is already member of 

the Employees’ Provident Fund ( Statutory 
Fund) or the Provident . Fund of another 
exempted establishment is employed in his 


establishment, the employer shall immediate- 
ly enrol him as a member of the Fund of the 
establishment, and accept the past accumula- 
tions in respect of such employee and credit 
to his account. 

6. The employer shall enhance the rate of provi- 

dent fund contribution appropriately if the 
rate of provident fund contributions for the 
class of establishments in which his establish- 
ment falls is enhanced under the Employees 7 
Provident Funds and Family Pension Fund 
Act 1952 so that the benefits under the provi- 
dent fund scheme of the establishment shall 
not become less favourable than the benefit 
provided under the Employees 7 Provident 
Funds and Family Pension Fund Act, 1952. 

7. The establishment shall submit an audited 

balance sheet of its provident fund every year 
to the Regional Provident Fund "Commis- 
sioner within 3 months of the close of the 
year. 

8. No amendment of the Rules of the provident 

fund shall be made without the previous ap- 
proval of the Central Provident Fund Com- 
missioner. Where any amendment is likely to 
effect adversely the interests of the employees, 
the Central Provident Fund Commissioner 
shall, before giving his approval, give a rea- 
sonable opportunity to the employees to 
explain their point of view,

8.0. 1195. — Whereas the Central Government was 
satisfied that, 

1. Kathiawar Ginning Factory & Oil Mill. 

2. The Kundla Press & Oil Mill P. Ltd. 

3. Saranjam Vibhag Khadi Karyalaya. 

4. Kajnl Oil Mill. 

5. Savarakundla Electric Supply Co. Lid. 

were situated in Savarkundla area which was a sparse 
area (that is, an area whose insurable population was 
less than 500; in the district of Bhavnagar in the State 
of Gujarat; 

And, whereas by virtue of their location in a sparse 
area, the aforesaid factories were granted exemption 
from the payment of the employer’s special contribu- 
tion under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insur- 
ance Act, 1948 (84 of 1948) until enforcement of the 
provisions of Chapter V of the said Act in that area 
by the Central Government in the notificalijn of the 
Government of India in the late Ministry of Labour 
and Employment No. S.O. 137, dated the 9th January, 
1962: 

And, whereas the Central Government is satisfied 
that tb e insurable population of the Savarkundla area 
in the district of Bhavnagar in the State of Gujarat 
has now exceeded 500, and it is no longer a sparse 
area; 

Now, therefore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act, 
1948 (34 of 1948), the Central Government hereby 
makes the following further amendment in the said 
notification, namely: — 

In the Schedule to the said notification, against 
Serial No. 5. the entry "Savarkundla” In 
column 3 and the corresponding entries in 
columns 4 and shall he omitted

S.O. 1396. —Whereas the Central Government was 
satisfied that: 

(1) Messrs Kalyan Rice and Oil Mills. 

(2) Messrs Shree Ganesh Rice and Oil Mills. 

(3) Messrs Tezpur Elect, and Engineering Com- 
pany Limited. 

(4) Messrs Steel Works Limited Factory No, 2. 

(5) Messrs Stale Transport Workshop, 

(6) Messrs Simpson and Company. 

(7) Messrs Tezpur Roller and Flour Mills. 

(8) Messrs Krishna Rice and Oil Mills. 

(9) Messrs Ashoka Motors. 

(10) Messrs Bhamrah Brothers: 

were situated in Tezpur area which was a sparse area 
(that is an area whose insurable population was less 
than 500) in the district of Darang in the State of 
Assam, 

And, whereas by virtue of their location in a sparse 
area, the aforesaid factories were granted exemption 
from the payment of the employer’s special contribu- 
tion under section 73F of the Employees’ State Insur- 
ance Act, 1948 (34 of 1948) until enforcement of the 
provisions of Chapter V of the said Art in that area 
by the Central Government in the notification of the 
Government of India in the late Ministry of Labour 
and Employment No, S.O, 845, dated the 16th March, 
1962. 

And. wherear the Central Government is satisfied 
that the insurable population of the Tezpur area in the 
district of Darang in the State of Assam has now 
exceeding 500, and it is no longer a sparse area: 

Now there Tore, in exercise of the powers conferred 
by section 73F of the Employees’ State Insurance Act 
1948 (34 of 1948), the C^iitral Government hereby 
makes ihe following further amendment in the said 
notification namely:' — ■ 

In the Schedule to the said notification against 
Serial No. 2 the entry “Tezpur” in column 3 
and the corresponding entries in column 4 
shall be omitted.

S.O. 1225. — Whereas the Central Government being 
satisfied that the public interest so required, had de- 
clared by a notification made in pursuance of the 
provisions of the proviso to sub-clause (vi) of clause 
(n) of section 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1047 
(14 of 1947), [being the notification of the Government 
of India, in the Ministry of Labour and Rehabilitation 
(Department of Labour and Employment) No. S.O. 
3829, dated the 13th S_eptember, 1971] the copper 
mining industry, to be a public utility service for the 
purposes of the said Act for a period of six months 
from the 26th September, 1971; 

And whereas the Central Government is of opinion 
that the public interest requires the extension of the 
said period by a further period of six months; 

Now, therefore, in exerpise of the powers conferred 
by the proviso to sub-clause (vi) or clause (n) of 
section 2 of the Industrial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 
1947), the Central Government hereby declare® the 
said industry to be a public utility service for the 
purposes of the said Act for a further period of six 
months from th© 25th March, 1972,

S.O. 1229* — In pursuance of section 17 of the Indus- 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central 
Government hereby publishes the following award of 
the Central Government Industrial Tribunal, Calcutta, 
in the industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to the management of Bankola Colliery. Post 
Office Ukhra, District Burdwan and their workmen, 
which was received by the Central Government on the 
29th April, 1972. 


BEFORE THE CENTRAL GOVERNMENT INDUS- 
TRIAL TRIBUNAL AT CALCUTTA. 

Reference No. 43 of 1971 

Parties: 

Employers in relation to the management of Ban- 
kola Colliery, 

and 

Their workmen. 

Present: 

Sri S. N. Bagchi Presiding Officer. 


2. Today is the second dale fixed for recording the 
compromise in ill o dispute under adjudication. The 
memorandum of compromise has been filed, signed for 
the employer by one S. P. Sharnia, duly constituted 
attorney of the Equitable Coal Company Limited. For 
the workman one J. Panel ty. General Secretary, Col- 
liery Mazdoor Congress Unci.), has signed. The cause 
of the workmen was espoused by the union and not 
by the General Secretary. It would have been proper 
and legal if the endorsement would have appeared as 
for the union representing the workmen through J. 
Pandey, General Secretary of the Union. But the 
endorsement that appears points out us if for workmen 
the General Secretary of the Colliery Mazdoor Con- 
gress (Ind) is representing. That cannot be the cor- 
rect legal position. 

3, However, if I am to consider this compromise, I 
cannot do so unless the union appears representing 
the workmen through anv of its officers, Mr. H. R. 
Das Gupta, Personnel Officer, appearing for the man- 
agement submit 'cd that the workmen concerned had 
been enjoying the benefits to which they were entitled 
under the terms of compromise. But the Tribunal 
cannot record the terms and effect an award thereon 
unless both the parties come and ask the tribunal lo 
record the compromise as in the memorandum of 
compromise and to pass an award forming the memo- 
randum of compromise a part of the award. 


Appearances ; 

On behalf of Workmen 

Sri J. Sharan, Personnel Officer. 

On behalf of Workmen 

Sri Benarashi Singh Azad, Genl. Secretary, Khan 
Shramik Congress. 

State: West Bengal iNDusTRy: Coal Mines 


AWARD 


By Order No. 6/46/70-LRII, dated 4th March, 1971, 
the Government of India, in the Ministry of Labour, 
Employment and Rehabilitation (Department of Lab- 
our and Employment), referred the following indus- 
trial dispute existing between the employers in rela- 
tion to the management of Bankola Colliefy and their 
workmen, to this Tribunal, for adjudication, namely: 


Whether the management of Bankola Colliery of 
Messrs Burrakur Coal Company Limited, Post 
Office Ukhra, District Burdwan was justified 
in stopping from work Sarvashri Sunit Das, 
Narayan Das, Bharat Roy, Tilok Gope, Jakir 
Hosen, Govind Nandi, Moheswar Jha, Clean- 
ing Mazdoors from 2nd June, 1970 ahd Shri 
Ajit Roy. Cleaning Mazdoor from the 29th 
May, 1970? If not, to what relief these work- 
men are entitled?” 


4. This Tribunal on 10th April, 1972 passed the 
following orders and notified both the parties accord- 
ingly: 

“Since the Memorandum of settlement filed 
requires certain clarification, let the matter 
be fixed again on 28th April, 1972 for record- 
ing settlement or for some other order/orders 
as this Tribunal would deem fit and proper.” 

Unfortunately the union has not turned up in spite of 
notice and Iho management has turned up. As the 
Union does not appear, this tribunal considers that 
there is no dispute over the matter referred to it for 
adjudication. So, I render an award to the effect that 
there is u no dispute” over the matter referred to adju- 
dication by this Tribunal. 

This is my award. 


anu me muuii nave come xo a 
compromise over the matters referred to for adjudi- 
cation and have filed a joint memorandum of terms of 
compromise for recording and passing an award thereof 
and have prayed for including the terms of the memo- 
randum of compromise in the award, I have consi- 
dered the terms of compromise as recorded in the 
memorandum and heard the representatives of the 
parties, The terms are fair, just and equitable and 
are beneficial to .he interest of the workmen who 
were engaged by the contractors when the dispute 
arose but are now to be included in the list of subs- 
titute workers as maintained by the colliery under 
the certified Standing orders and would thereby have 
a chance of getting appointment directly in the coll- 
. Heiice the memorandum of compromise be re- 
corded and an award be rendered thereon making 
such memorandum a part of the award. s 


Dated, 

28th April, 1972, 


(Sd.) S. N. Baqchi, 

Presiding Officer. 
[No. L/l 91 2/1 3/7 1 -LRII. ] 


The reference is accordingly disposed of. 

(Sd) S. N. BAGCHI, 

Presiding Officer.

Between 

The Employers in relation to the Management of

and 

Their workmen represented by Khan Shramik 
Congress. 

The parties above-named most respectfully 
Sheweth: 

1. That the parties have mutually settled the above 
dispute on the following terms: 

(a) That the workmen named in the Schedule to 
the reference will be placed in Badli list from 
to-day and will be provided with work against 
badli vacancies under standing orders. 

(b) If and when any regular vacancies occur these 
persons will be absorbed in those vacancies 
instead of taking any new hands in those 
vacancies. 

(c) The parties will bear their own costs. 

The petitioners therefore pray that the Hon’ble 
Tribunal would be graciously pleased to 
pass an award in terms of the settlement 
mentioned above and for tnis the parties as 
duty bound will ever pray. 

This memorandum shall form part of the Award.

S.O. 1230.— In pursuance of section 17 of the Indus- 
trial Disputes Act, 1947 (14 of 1947), the Central Gov- 
ernment hereby publishes the following award of the 
Industrial Tribunal, Ahmedabad In the Industrial dis- 
pute between. the employers in relation to the manage- 
ment of Messrs Desmet India Private Limited, Ahmed- 
abad and their workmen, which was received by the 
Central Government on the 5th May, 1972. 

BEFORE SHRI INDERJIT G. THAKORE, PRESIDING 
OFFICER, INDUSTRIAL TRIBUNAL, GUJARAT 

Reference (ITC) No. 2 of 1971 

between 

Messrs Desmet (India) Private Limited, Ahmedabad. 

AND 

The ONGC Employees Union, Ahmedabad. 

In the matter of whether the Company was justified 
in denying the workmen work by closing down site at 
Navagam and Kalol. 

Appearances ; 

Shri R. S, Burihar, works manager for the company. 
Shri J. S. Parasar for the Union. 

AWARD 

This industrial dispute between the employers in 
relation to the management of Messrs Desmet India 
Private Ltd., Ahmedabad and their workmen in res- 
pect o i the matters specified in the Schedule has been 


referred to me for adjudication as Industrial Tribunal 
by the Government of India, Ministry of Labour 
Employment and Rehabilitation (Department of Labour 
and Employment) by their Order No. S.O. dated 5th 
June, 1971. The dispute relates to a number of matters 
mentioned in the Schedule to the said order. After 
this matter was fixed for hearing on numerous occasions 
I am glad the parties have come to terms and requested 
me to make an award in terms thereof, I, therefore* 
make an award In terms of settlement a copy of which 
is annexed hereto and marked Annexure “A". 

2 . Unde r paragraph 3 of the settlement it is agreed 
between the parties that as far as the workers at 
CTF. Oil Fields Kalol are concerned, the management 
shall make ex-crratia payment to the workers wages 
amounting to such number of days as may be directed 
by this Tribunal in full settlement of the demand in 
this respect. The Company is prepared to pay any 
amount whereas the union wants atleast a payment 
of one month and 22 days, I direct the concern that it 
shall under this clause make an ex-gratia payment of 
twenty two days wages. This disposes of the reference. 

Sd/- INDRAJIT G. THAKORE, 

Presiding Officer* Industrial Tribunal,

11. The story given by W.W.l ns to how the members

of 1971 appear to have been enrolled on 17th April,

nf the Sangh. There is nothing on record, to show that

CoTliery wi + h the approval of the Executive Committee 
of the Sangh.

14. Even if the receipts and the Register of member- 
ship are taken to be genuine, even then there is nothing 
on record to show that the present two disputes have 
been sponsored by the members of the union working 
in the Badjna Colliery or by the union itself. The law 
on this point has thus been laid down by the Supreme 
Court in workmen of Dharampal Prem Chand and 
Dharampai Premchand. (19651 I EX.J. 668: “In every 
case where industrial adjudication has to decide whether 
a reference in regard to the dismissal of an Industrial 
employee is validly made or not, it would always bo 
necessary to enquire whether the union which has 
sponsored the case can fairly claim a representative 
character In such a way that its support to the cause 

would make the dispute an industrial dispute 

(In) some cases the union of workmen working in one 
industry mRy be competent to raise a dispute about 
the wrongful dismissal of an employee engaged in an 
establishment belonging to the same industry where 
workmen in such an establishment have no union of 
their own, and an appreciable number of such work- 
men had Coined such other union before their dis- 
missal/' In the instant cases the Mine Karmachari 
Sangh, a general union appears to have espoused the 
cause of the four workmen stopped from working. The 
Colliery Mazdoor Sangh. the recognised union, has 
been functioning in the Badjna Colliery for a long time. 
The Colliery Mazdoor Sangh also appears to he a 
general union. So the workmen of the Bad jo a Colliery 
have no exclusive union of their own. The Mine 
Karmachari Sangh is no doubt competent to espouse 
the cause of the’ workmen stopped from work by th© 
management of Radina Colliery, but before doing so 
the union must establish that an appreciable number 
of the workmen of the Badjna Colliery had joined th© 
Mine Karmachari Sangh before the workmen concerned 
had been stopped from work. But all the four work- 
men, involved in the two references were stopped from 
work before an appreciable number of the workmen 
of the Badina Colliery joined the Mine Karmachari 
Sanph. Avaln, a question may pertinently be asked 
whether 249 out of more than 1500 workmen may be 
regarded as nn appreciable number. W.W.l ha? : saia 
lhat 1600 to 1700 workmen work in the Badjna Collterv, 
If that be 249 cannot be regarded as an appreciable 
number. Then again, there is nothing on record to 
show that even the Mine Karmachari Sangh has really 
espoused the cause of the four workmen stopped from 
work No resolution of the union has been produced 
to indicate that the union has taken up the cause ol 
the four workmen or that the union has authorised th© 
General Secretary to raise industrial disputes on behalf 
of the concerned workmen. 


15 In Bombav Union of Journalists and Tbe Hindu. 
Bnmhav, 1061 ( 2 ) ui. 436 the resolution authorising 

the r^neral union to espouse the couse of the dis- 
missed workman was found to be a fabrication. The 
Supreme Court, therefore, came to Ihe conclusion that 
the union was not competent to espouse the caus* of 
the dismissed workmen. Tn the instant, two cases there 
no resolution whatsoever authorising th© union to 
take up the cause of the workmen stopped from work- 
ing. 


16, In Nelli! Cotton Mills and Labour Court, Madura 
965 (I) Ti.L.J. P.95, the dispute of an individual 

Worker was taken up and referred fm- *d Indication 
t th" Instance of a general union nf which some or 
ho follow workman in th" establishment were m embers, 
’ho Madras F1?h Uourt, held in that case that the 
art that a substantial number of workmen from the 
stahlishment in which tbe concerned workman wo* 
rrmloved were also membprp of the union would not 
v itself be sufficient tn concert the individual dispute 
ito an Industrial dispute. It must further be shown 
hserved the Madras High Court, that they participated 
t or a ©ted together and arrived at an under standing nv 
1 resolution or hv other means and ^Uectlvely sup- 
orted on the date of the reference the demand or 
ause of the Individual worker. This principle holds 
r ood in the case of a collactlv* dispute even after th* 



THE GAZETTE OF INDIA: MAY 20, 1072/VAISAKHA 30, 1894 


[Part u— 


1872 


insertion of Sec. 2A which says that an individual dis- 
pute shaii be deemed to be an industrial dispute, In 
the instant cases I am to consider whether there are 
collective disputes between the employers and their 
workmen. There is nothing on record to show that the 
alleged members of the Mine Karmachari Sangh work- 
ing in the Badina Colliery participated in or acted 
together and arrived at an understanding by a resolu- 
tion or by other means and collectively supported on 
the date of the reference the demand or cause of the 
individual workmen involved in the instant two 
references* 


17* In Murugappa Mudaliar, Rathina Mudaliar & Sons 
and Raju Mudaliar & Others 1965 (I) L.L.J. 489 the 
following test was laid down for ascertaining when 
an individual dispute becomes a collective disDute: 
"From these decisions it is clear that an individual 
dispute can become an ‘industrial dispute* only if the 
grievance of an employee is taken up by his fellow 
workers or a. substantial section of them working und°r 
the same employer ” The fart that his grievance has 
been *aken up by a union, consisting of workmen having 
no interest in his dispute, cannot convert that dispute 
into an 'industrial dispute'. In the instant cases there 
Is nothing on record to show that t^e grievance of the 
four employees stopped from work has been taken 
up by their fellow workers or a substantial section of 
them working under the same employer, Even assum- 
ing that the Mine Karmachari Sangh. has raised the 
disputes on behalf of the aggrieved workmen, it is not 
known how this union espoused the cause of the ag- 
grieved workmen whether by a resolution or by any 
other means. If by a resolution it is not known how 
manv of the fellow workmen participated in the said 
resolution. This union cannot ask for reference unless 
it is requested to do so by such of its members as are 
employed in the Badjna Collierv. As the validity of 
the two references has been challenged by the manage- 
ment, it was the duty of the union to supply materials 
to satisfy the Tribunal that the references have been 
valldlv made in respect of collective disputes. But no 
such materials have been supplied by the union. 

18. The foregoing discussion makes it clear that each 
one of the two references relate to an individual, as 
apposed to a collective dispute. But the references 
purport to be in respective of colleoMve disputes, that 
is to sav, in respect of disputes between the workmen 
of the Badina Collierv and their employers* As the 
existence of auv collective dispute between the 
employers and their workmen has not been established 
in either of the two references* the only award that 
can be given in each of these two r°ferences Is of a 
'no depute’ award, and I award accordingly in each 
one of the two references heard together. 

19. A copy of this award may be forwarded to the 
Central Government under section 15 of the Industrial 
Disputes Act, 1947.
